Read [the status contract](references/contract.md) when creating or changing a plan. Keep `task.completion_gate` tied to accepted business output.\n3. Initialize the task with `scripts/task_status.py init --root <repo> --plan <plan.json>`. Never use `--force`; resume an existing task instead of replacing its ledger. Initialization automatically registers `<repo>` with the local Long Task Control Agent when it is installed. In Codex, initialization also records the stable current thread ID from `CODEX_THREAD_ID`/`CODEX_SESSION_ID`; the hub can then follow later Codex task renames without guessing from title text.\n4. When the workstation Agent is installed, use its automatically maintained, authenticated Task Status Hub. It rereads registered workspace roots on each refresh, survives logon/reboot, and supplies its LAN link to the central dashboard. Do not start a second manual server on the same port. Without an installed Agent, run `scripts/task_status.py serve --root <repo> --open` in a retained local-only process. See [adoption](references/adoption.md).\n5. Tell the user the task ID and available local or central status entry after the first snapshot exists.\n\nWhen resuming an existing task, run `snapshot --root <repo> --task-id <id>` before substantive work. Besides rebuilding the projection, this refreshes the local Agent registration. Agent absence or registration failure must not prevent local task tracking; report a warning and continue with the ledger.\n\nIf a task is accepted but should not start yet, leave it in `planned` or move it to `ready`. An admitted phone business task is eligible for the formal queue even before its first phone call; generic ledgers remain visible as other/excluded records. Set a durable recommendation order with `queue-position --position <n>`, where smaller positive numbers appear first, or use `--clear` to return to priority/time ordering. Queue order is advisory: the dashboard never starts, pauses, or interrupts Codex work. Start a step only when execution actually begins.\n\nUse `assets/example-plan.json` as a shape reference, not as a mandatory phase list. Adapt phase and step names to the engineering work.\n\n## Report progress while working\n\n- Start a leaf step before performing it:\n  `scripts/task_status.py step-start --root <repo> --task-id <id> --step-id <step> --action <text> --next-action <text>`\n- Complete it only when its acceptance condition has evidence:\n  `scripts/task_status.py step-complete --root <repo> --task-id <id> --step-id <step> --evidence <ref>`\n- For an active step longer than about two minutes, emit a heartbeat at least every 60–120 seconds with the current action and next action.\n- A heartbeat proves liveness only. Complete a leaf step or advance an evidence-backed workflow stage as soon as its acceptance gate passes; otherwise the central panel will correctly treat a long-running task with fresh heartbeats but no verified progress as potentially stalled.\n- When the task repeatedly operates an external product, or the user asks for detailed efficiency analysis, record operation-level timing as described in [the status contract](references/contract.md). Keep plan steps at outcome/gate granularity; put checks, file staging/upload, picker selection, prompt relay, readback, Send, post-send verification, downloads, validation, promotion, cleanup, and waits in episode operation records. Capture each operation's real start/end or measured elapsed time at execution time. Never reconstruct a missing subduration by evenly dividing an episode total.\n- When many comparable business units pass through a multi-stage workflow, use the `workflow-stage` contract in [the status contract](references/contract.md). Update a stage only from evidence for that exact gate. A completed model/chat reply may advance a reply stage, but it must not silently advance capture, validation, publication, promotion, or the task completion gate. Declare the stage that represents accepted business output as authoritative so the panel does not mistake a coarse plan step or a reply count for final progress.\n- Keep one current leaf step per task. Represent genuine parallel work as separate tasks so timing and ownership stay legible in the portfolio.\n- Adjust only `planned` or `ready` tasks with `queue-position`; every change is an append-only `queue_reordered` event. Do not rewrite plan files or ledger history to reorder work.\n- Record external waits, blockers, and human checkpoints immediately with `update`; do not hide them inside prose or inflate progress while waiting. Use `waiting_external` before a model, device, build, or remote service enters a naturally long wait so the health monitor does not mistake an expected wait for stalled execution.\n- Use `update --status running --clear-blocker` when verified work resumes.\n- Use `finish --status completed` only after the task completion gate passes. Include at least one evidence reference. Use `failed` or `cancelled` only for genuine terminal outcomes.\n\nThe dashboard derives progress from completed weighted steps. Never write a subjective percentage.\n\n## Keep the record trustworthy\n\n- Treat `events.jsonl` as append-only. Do not hand-edit or truncate it.\n- Treat `task.origin.codex_thread_id` as an immutable identity link, not a display title. The hub may render the current Codex task title and retain the plan title as `ledger_title`; never infer or rewrite an identity link merely because two titles look similar.\n- Separate producer business status from observed execution. A Codex turn ending, aborting, failing, or going stale does not finish, cancel, fail, or advance the business task. Fresh, exact-target ACTIVE scheduler metadata may explain an ended turn as `waiting_schedule`; stale, paused, offline, missing, or ambiguous scheduler evidence does not establish normal waiting. Only record business terminal outcomes explicitly through `finish`.\n- Old `completed` snapshots without usable terminal ledger proof are completion-unverified, not gate-passed history. Keep them visible and verify the original evidence; do not fabricate a terminal event or rewrite history to make a card look complete.\n- Use the CLI for every transition so sequence numbers, timestamps, timing buckets, and `snapshot.json` remain consistent.\n- Preserve separate active, waiting, and idle time. Waiting is a real state, not missing progress.\n- Treat the panel's execution-health label as a derived diagnostic, not a producer state. “Normal execution” means heartbeats are fresh and the no-progress grace period has not expired; it is not proof that work advanced. “Suspected stall”, “lost heartbeat”, “needs human”, and “abnormal” require checking the evidence and recording the appropriate explicit task transition.\n- Label operation timing as `exact`, `approximate`, `best-known`, or `unknown`. If durable evidence proves an operation happened but not how long it took, retain the operation with `unknown` precision so the dashboard says “未单独计时”. Phase spans and inner measurements may overlap; do not sum overlapping values into invented wall time.\n- A plan change must be explicit: update the plan through the CLI's `replan` command with a reason, then continue from the projected state.\n- Before handing off or ending a turn, emit a final heartbeat or state transition that names the next action.\n- Runtime state belongs under `artifacts/task-status/` by default and should remain gitignored. Commit the reusable skill, not live task ledgers.\n\n## Notify when work stops\n\nWhen the user requires DingTalk notification for stopped long tasks, read [DingTalk stop alerts](references/dingtalk.md). Formal phone-task notifications and progress counts require admitted business scope; candidate/excluded records do not qualify merely because their source says `running` or `needs_human`. Keep notification mode in shadow until delivery is explicitly authorized and the watcher supports the admission contract. The watcher deduplicates incidents and retries delivery failures.\n\nIf periodic progress feedback is enabled, use an explicit notification timezone and quiet-hours window rather than the workstation timezone. update、`wall_diagnostics` 和可得 active-map/state trace 的对应键与时间；不得把 current update 缺行解释为完整状态不变。\n4. diagnostics 为空时只报告“在已核范围内未观察到诊断”；不得证明异常路径正确。\n5. 若解码会触发大加载、隐式计算、未知写入或内存风险，立即停止在 metadata 层并记录原因。\n\n### E. 无可用材料时的运行预检——禁止实际运行\n\n若 C/D 不能取得可用材料，只调查并报告：\n\n- 可执行入口和精确命令模板；\n- 最小可区分窗口/对象的候选来源（历史 fixture、已知短窗或现有 receipt），以及是否必然重算 WallAssembler/上游；\n- 预计文件/行数、内存/耗时的证据来源与不确定性；\n- 需要的环境、日历、参数、数据权限；\n- 安全停止点和可观察回执；\n- 为什么该最小动作足以区分当前问题。\n\n不得实际启动该运行。是否采用窗口及是否通过 G-RUN 由 E1/manager 后继决定。\n\n## 5. 输出与文件合同\n\n在唯一写面中生成：\n\n```text\n/dwdhome/fy/BASE_E6_2/.oi-runs/OI-EAW-WALL-20261114-01/D001/W001/\n├── Queue order is advisory: the dashboard never starts, pauses, or interrupts Codex work. Start a step only when execution actually begins.\n\nUse `assets/example-plan.json` as a shape reference, not as a mandatory phase list. Adapt phase and step names to the engineering work.\n\n## Report progress while working\n\n- Start a leaf step before performing it:\n  `scripts/task_status.py step-start --root <repo> --task-id <id> --step-id <step> --action <text> --next-action <text>`\n- Complete it only when its acceptance condition has evidence:\n  `scripts/task_status.py step-complete --root <repo> --task-id <id> --step-id <step> --evidence <ref>`\n- For an active step longer than about two minutes, emit a heartbeat at least every 60–120 seconds with the current action and next action.\n- A heartbeat proves liveness only. Complete a leaf step or advance an evidence-backed workflow stage as soon as its acceptance gate passes; otherwise the central panel will correctly treat a long-running task with fresh heartbeats but no verified progress as potentially stalled.\n- When the task repeatedly operates an external product, or the user asks for detailed efficiency analysis, record operation-level timing as described in [the status contract](references/contract.md). Keep plan steps at outcome/gate granularity; put checks, file staging/upload, picker selection, prompt relay, readback, Send, post-send verification, downloads, validation, promotion, cleanup, and waits in episode operation records. Capture each operation's real start/end or measured elapsed time at execution time. Never reconstruct a missing subduration by evenly dividing an episode total.\n- When many comparable business units pass through a multi-stage workflow, use the `workflow-stage` contract in [the status contract](references/contract.md). Update a stage only from evidence for that exact gate. A completed model/chat reply may advance a reply stage, but it must not silently advance capture, validation, publication, promotion, or the task completion gate. Declare the stage that represents accepted business output as authoritative so the panel does not mistake a coarse plan step or a reply count for final progress.\n- Keep one current leaf step per task. Represent genuine parallel work as separate tasks so timing and ownership stay legible in the portfolio.\n- Adjust only `planned` or `ready` tasks with `queue-position`; every change is an append-only `queue_reordered` event. Do not rewrite plan files or ledger history to reorder work.\n- Record external waits, blockers, and human checkpoints immediately with `update`; do not hide them inside prose or inflate progress while waiting. Use `waiting_external` before a model, device, build, or remote service enters a naturally long wait so the health monitor does not mistake an expected wait for stalled execution.\n- Use `update --status running --clear-blocker` when verified work resumes.\n- Use `finish --status completed` only after the task completion gate passes. Include at least one evidence reference. Use `failed` or `cancelled` only for genuine terminal outcomes.\n\nThe dashboard derives progress from completed weighted steps. Never write a subjective percentage.\n\n## Keep the record trustworthy\n\n- Treat `events.jsonl` as append-only. Do not hand-edit or truncate it.\n- Treat `task.origin.codex_thread_id` as an immutable identity link, not a display title. The hub may render the current Codex task title and retain the plan title as `ledger_title`; never infer or rewrite an identity link merely because two titles look similar.\n- Separate producer business status from observed execution. A Codex turn ending, aborting, failing, or going stale does not finish, cancel, fail, or advance the business task. Fresh, exact-target ACTIVE scheduler metadata may explain an ended turn as `waiting_schedule`; stale, paused, offline, missing, or ambiguous scheduler evidence does not establish normal waiting. Only record business terminal outcomes explicitly through `finish`.\n- Old `completed` snapshots without usable terminal ledger proof are completion-unverified, not gate-passed history. Keep them visible and verify the original evidence; do not fabricate a terminal event or rewrite history to make a card look complete.\n- Use the CLI for every transition so sequence numbers, timestamps, timing buckets, and `snapshot.json` remain consistent.\n- Preserve separate active, waiting, and idle time. Waiting is a real state, not missing progress.\n- Treat the panel's execution-health label as a derived diagnostic, not a producer state. “Normal execution” means heartbeats are fresh and the no-progress grace period has not expired; it is not proof that work advanced. “Suspected stall”, “lost heartbeat”, “needs human”, and “abnormal” require checking the evidence and recording the appropriate explicit task transition.\n- Label operation timing as `exact`, `approximate`, `best-known`, or `unknown`. If durable evidence proves an operation happened but not how long it took, retain the operation with `unknown` precision so the dashboard says “未单独计时”. Phase spans and inner measurements may overlap; do not sum overlapping values into invented wall time.\n- A plan change must be explicit: update the plan through the CLI's `replan` command with a reason, then continue from the projected state.\n- Before handing off or ending a turn, emit a final heartbeat or state transition that names the next action.\n- Runtime state belongs under `artifacts/task-status/` by default and should remain gitignored. Commit the reusable skill, not live task ledgers.\n\n## Notify when work stops\n\nWhen the user requires DingTalk notification for stopped long tasks, read [DingTalk stop alerts](references/dingtalk.md). Formal phone-task notifications and progress counts require admitted business scope; candidate/excluded records do not qualify merely because their source says `running` or `needs_human`. Keep notification mode in shadow until delivery is explicitly authorized and the watcher supports the admission contract. The watcher deduplicates incidents and retries delivery failures.\n\nIf periodic progress feedback is enabled, use an explicit notification timezone and quiet-hours window rather than the workstation timezone. Progress digests must distinguish completed-step/weight changes from heartbeat-only activity; a fresh heartbeat is liveness evidence, not completion evidence.\n\nNever place a robot webhook, access token, or signing secret in a tracked file, task plan, ledger event, command line, or user-visible output. Use the gitignored runtime config or environment variables. Validate with a dry run before enabling delivery; a live test message is an external side effect and requires user authorization.\n\nBefore ending a turn because work is blocked or needs user action, record that state through the status CLI so the watcher has an immediate, trustworthy trigger. The watcher is still required because it is what detects a producer that disappears without recording a final transition.\n\nWhen several machines or phones can produce long tasks, assign stable human-readable `task.origin.machine` and `task.origin.phone` aliases in each runtime plan, plus `task.origin.phone_adb_ip` when a current ADB target is configured. The DingTalk watcher uses local config fallbacks, while task-specific origin values take precedence. It is an aggregate, CLI-maintained projection of evidence-backed business stages; it does not replace the event ledger or completion gate.\n\n## Plan schema\n\nRequired top-level fields:\n\n- `schema_version`: currently `1`.\n- `project`: `id` and human-readable `title`.\n- `task`: `id`, `title`, and `completion_gate`; `type`, `priority`, `owner`, `queue_position`, and `origin` are optional. `queue_position` is a positive integer used only while the task is `planned` or `ready`; smaller values are displayed first. `origin.machine` and `origin.phone` are human-readable execution aliases for notifications and portfolio context. `origin.phone_adb_ip` is an optional IPv4 or IPv6 address identifying the currently configured phone; resolve it from the current product runtime, never from history. `origin.codex_thread_id` is the stable identity of the Codex task that owns the ledger. New tasks initialized inside Codex record it automatically from `CODEX_THREAD_ID`/`CODEX_SESSION_ID`; older tasks may add it only after verifying the creating thread. Runtime origins stay gitignored and must not contain tokens, MAC addresses, or other secrets.\n- `phases`: ordered phases containing ordered steps.\n\nFor an accepted long-running phone business goal, include this task field as part of normal planning, without asking the user to register monitoring separately:\n\n```json\n\"monitoring\": {\"scope\": \"phone_long_task\"}\n```\n\n`task.monitoring` is optional for other ledgers; when present it must be an object with `scope` exactly `phone_long_task` or `general`. Invalid values (including null, missing scope, whitespace variants, arrays, and booleans) are rejected. `general` explicitly excludes a ledger from formal phone business even if its type looks mobile-related. Do not declare phone business from incidental phone tests. `init` and reasoned `replan` retain the scope in the accepted plan and plan hash; `replan` retains an omitted stable thread ID and rejects a different or cleared one. Older unlinked ledgers may acquire an identity only after independently verifying their owning root thread.\n\nEvery step requires a task-unique `id`, `title`, `acceptance`, and positive numeric `weight`. IDs use lowercase letters, digits, dots, underscores, or hyphens. A useful leaf step normally represents 5–30 minutes of active work or one externally observable wait boundary.\n\nThe plan is detailed storage, not the default presentation. The hub keeps every phase and acceptance condition available but expands details only on demand. Use enough leaf steps to make the current position and next gate obvious; do not split work into keystroke-level activity.\n\nDo not force every project into one phase vocabulary. Examples:\n\n- Software: scope, design, implementation, tests, review, release.\n- Research: scope, sources, analysis, synthesis, verification, delivery.\n- Mobile generation: freeze input, submit, wait, recover, validate, promote.\n\n## Status vocabulary\n\n- `planned`: plan exists but execution has not started.\n- `ready`: prerequisites are satisfied.\n- `running`: active work is happening.\n- `waiting_external`: an external system is working or an expected artifact has not arrived.\n- `blocked`: work cannot proceed without resolving a problem.\n- `needs_human`: a material decision, permission, or manual action is required.\n- `completed`, `failed`, `cancelled`: terminal states.\n\nStep states are `planned`, `running`, `waiting_external`, `blocked`, `needs_human`, `completed`, and `skipped`.\n\nAt portfolio level, admission comes before business grouping. Admitted `planned` and `ready` tasks are formal queue entries, including declared phone business before any phone call. Admitted `completed` with verified terminal proof, and producer-owned `failed`/`cancelled`, are business history. Automatic candidates and other/excluded ledgers stay visible in their own entry points, never in formal counts just because their raw status looks active or completed. Queue order is an execution recommendation, not a remote command channel. Explicit `queue_position` sorts first, then task priority, creation time, machine, and title.\n\nVersion 1 models one current leaf step per task. Use separate tasks for genuinely parallel work streams; this keeps progress, timing, ownership, and handoff unambiguous. A portfolio can contain any number of concurrent tasks across repositories.\n\n## Event rules\n\nEach event contains a monotonically increasing `seq`, ISO-8601 `at`, `type`, and task state changes relevant to that event. Evidence is a list of file paths, test names, URLs, message IDs, or other stable references.\n\nEvent timestamps must not move backwards. A completed or skipped step is immutable, and a terminal task accepts no further events. Start a step before completing it, and finish or skip the current active step before starting another one.\n\nThe CLI supports these event families:\n\n- `initialized` and `replanned` for plan lifecycle.\n- `queue_reordered` for setting or clearing `queue_position` while a task is `planned` or `ready`.\n- `step_started` and `step_completed` for measurable progress.\n- `status_updated` for waiting, blocking, resuming, and human checkpoints.\n- `heartbeat` for freshness without changing state.\n- `task_finished` for a terminal outcome.\n\nProgress is `completed step weight / total step weight`. A task may report 100% step completion while still running its final completion gate; it is complete only after an explicit terminal event.\n\n## Business workflow stage progress\n\nUse workflow stages when a task repeatedly moves comparable units through several independently verifiable gates, such as request sent, model reply complete, response captured, artifact validated, and artifact published. Stage names are task-defined; do not hardcode a particular book, chapter, model, or product.\n\nThe first `workflow-stage` update creates `workflow-progress.json` beside the task snapshot and requires `--total`, `--unit`, `--stage-title`, and `--authoritative`. Later calls update one stage atomically:\n\n```text\nscripts/task_status.py workflow-stage --root <repo> --task-id <task> \\\n  --stage-id accepted --stage-title \"Accepted output\" --completed 36 \\\n  --failed 22 --waiting 5 --total 63 --unit item --authoritative \\\n  --source \"strict validator and canonical store\" --evidence artifacts/validation-summary.csv\n```\n\nEach stage has disjoint `completed`, `failed`, `in_progress`, and `waiting` counts; `pending` is derived so the counts total the declared scope. Evidence should identify the exact chats, reports, validation summaries, artifacts, or immutable result IDs supporting the count. Do not advance a later gate from an earlier signal:\n\n- A successful Send proves only request submission.\n- A completed/idle chat turn with a verified assistant response proves only reply completion.\n- A copied or downloaded result proves capture or availability, not validation.\n- A self-reported model status is not an independent validation pass.\n- Validation or canonical/published state requires its own durable evidence.\n\nThe authoritative stage is the task-specific acceptance stage used for the panel headline and progress notifications. Other stages remain visible in the expanded pipeline so operators can distinguish model throughput from accepted business output. Weighted plan progress remains available for plan gates and timing; workflow progress does not authorize `finish --status completed` unless the task completion gate and all plan steps also pass.\n\nTiming is reconstructed from state intervals and attributed to the active leaf step:\n\n- `running` contributes to declared active time. It is a producer state, not proof that CPU, Codex, or the phone continuously made substantive progress.\n- `waiting_external`, `blocked`, and `needs_human` contribute to separate waiting buckets as well as total waiting time.\n- `planned` and `ready` contribute to idle time.\n\nAt task level these buckets are mutually exclusive. Subject only to per-field rounding:\n\n`elapsed_seconds = accounted_seconds + unobserved_seconds`, where\n`accounted_seconds = active_seconds + waiting_external_seconds + blocked_seconds + needs_human_seconds + idle_seconds`.\nThe interval ending at an event is charged using the state already in effect before that event; the event's new state starts at its own timestamp.\n\nThe central monitor also derives execution health without changing the ledger. Ordinary heartbeats and process presence are liveness evidence only. The last substantive-progress time comes from completed/skipped steps, evidence-backed workflow-stage updates, and verified operations for automatically discovered phone tasks. A fresh heartbeat with no such progress beyond the configured stall threshold becomes “suspected stall”; a stale heartbeat becomes “lost execution”. Explicit `waiting_external` is excluded from stall alerts.\n\nEach projected step exposes the same timing buckets for efficiency analysis. Task-level `unassigned_seconds` is a\nsubset of accounted time and records intervals before or between active steps; never add it to task elapsed time. For a non-terminal task, time after the latest ledger event is\n`unobserved_seconds`; it is not silently counted as active work. Regular heartbeats therefore keep accounted time\ncurrent, while a disappeared producer cannot inflate execution time indefinitely. A heartbeat closes an observed\nstate interval but is liveness evidence only; it does not itself prove substantive progress.\n\n## Episode and atomic-operation timing\n\nPlan steps describe accepted outcomes and state-machine gates. They deliberately do not represent every click. A task that needs detailed efficiency analysis may add a gitignored `operation-timing-source.json` beside its snapshot. Long Task Control then exposes a nested task → episode → operation view without adding business-specific fields to the task plan.\n\nThe current source contract is:\n\n```json\n{\n  \"schema_version\": 1,\n  \"source\": {\n    \"format\": \"episode_csv_v1\",\n    \"path\": \"artifacts/<business-area>/timing-ledger.csv\"\n  },\n  \"limits\": { \"max_episodes\": 200 }\n}\n```\n\nThe source path is relative to the repository root and must stay inside it. `max_episodes` is bounded to `1..500`. The CSV is append-only and has these columns:\n\n```text\nepisode_id,namespace,chapter,phase,start_at,end_at,elapsed_seconds,time_precision,relay_seconds,critical_action_seconds,product_work_seconds,logging_closeout_seconds,status,issue_codes,evidence_path\n```\n\n`namespace`, `chapter`, and `phase` are generic grouping labels; a software task may use component, change, and build/test/release instead. `evidence_path` points to an immutable repository-local episode report. Put `Episode:` in the report header and, for new runs, use a standard detailed section:\n\n```markdown\n## Operation timing\n\n| Operation | Category | Status | Started at | Finished at | Elapsed seconds | Evidence/result |\n|---|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---|\n| Check current UI and predecessor gate | check | completed | 2026-09-02T12:27:15.010Z | 2026-09-02T12:27:19.241Z | 4.231 s | fresh screenshot + tuple proof |\n| Stage PDF for upload | upload | completed | 2026-09-02T12:27:19.241Z | 2026-09-02T12:28:38.699Z | 79.458 s | staged once, replacement disabled |\n| Relay prompt | prompt_relay | completed | 2026-09-02T12:36:46.660Z | 2026-09-02T12:36:53.310Z | 6.650 s | one relay invocation |\n| Read back prompt fingerprint | readback | completed | 2026-09-02T12:36:53.310Z | 2026-09-02T12:37:01.004Z | 7.694 s | exact bytes/units/SHA-256 |\n| Click Send and reconcile ACK | send | completed | 2026-09-02T12:37:58.112Z | 2026-09-02T12:38:16.336Z | 18.224 s | one touch;
